However, Testimony 2 claims there was no sign of the president getting on the elevator at all, which could imply he might not have used the elevator to come down.

**Step 2:** Checking if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction, we find Prop 3 relevant. It states, "If the elevator was the only way for President Wang to get down from the roof then President Wang was inside the elevator if he was not on the roof." This proposition directly addresses the possibility of President Wang's descent from the roof.

**Step 3:** Prop 3 is applicable because it discusses the necessity of the elevator for descending from the roof. If the elevator was indeed the only way down, and there's no record of President Wang using it (as per Testimony 2), this creates a contradiction with the implication that he must have been inside the elevator if he wasn't on the roof (since he had to get down somehow). However, the critical contradiction arises when considering Testimony 3, which states there's no other way for the president to get down without using the elevator, and Testimony 4, which jokingly suggests being pushed off the roof as an alternative. The actual contradiction we're focusing on involves Evidence 2 (Bigg Building) and Testimony 3, as the building's layout and the existence of other potential means of descent (like stairs, even if not mentioned, or being pushed off, as jokingly suggested) are not directly addressed by the testimonies but are implied by the evidence of the building's structure.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning used here involves understanding the layout of the Bigg Building and the implications of the testimonies about President Wang's descent. The contradiction lies in the assumption that the elevator is the only means of descent, which might not be true given the building's structure and potential alternative routes or means of exit (intentional or unintentional, like falling or being pushed).

**Step 5:** Given the analysis, the contradiction between the assumption of the elevator being the sole means of descent and the potential for other means (as suggested by the building's structure and the joking testimony about being pushed) leads us to identify a specific pair of evidence and testimony that highlights this inconsistency.
